) prohibit the appearance of other current-current interaction terms under the RG flow.

We shall now directly compute the 2-loop
\beta
-function for the coupling
{\tilde{\lambda}}
of the truncated
\sigma
-model (
4.20
) in large
{\tilde{k}}
perturbation theory, demonstrating its renormalizability with only
{\tilde{\lambda}}
(or
\lambda
) running. Let us emphasize that — modulo a finite renormalization, or redefinition, of
\lambda
encoded in the choice of the coefficient
{\rm q}
in (
4.7
) — the RG evolution of the tripled
\sigma
-model (
4.17
)
is exactly equivalent to that of the
\lambda
-model (
4.15
), since they were related at the level of the path integral including the necessary Jacobian (
4.7
).
61
61

61

The

\beta
-function of the truncated
G\times G
model (
4.20
) can actually be extracted from that of the more general
G\times G
model studied in Chapter
3
(given explicitly in App.
D.1
). For completeness, we explicitly run through the computation again here. The results are in agreement those in App.
D.1
for the special values of the parameters in (
4.21
).

Let us introduce the basis
\{T_{A}\}
for
{\rm Lie}(G)
(see Appendix
A
for conventions).
We shall use the indices
A
and
\bar{A}
respectively for the two

G
-valued fields
{\hat{h}}
and
\bar{h}
in (
4.20
) (with the tangent space index for
G\times G
denoted as

M=\{A,\bar{A}\}
). As in

[
192
]
we introduce the vielbein

\displaystyle E^{M}=(e^{A},e^{\bar{A}})=(\sqrt{1-{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}\,{J}^{A},\,{K}^{\bar{A}}+{\tilde{\lambda}}{J}^{A})\ ,

(4.24)

\displaystyle{J}\equiv T_{A}{J}^{A}={\hat{h}}^{-1}d{\hat{h}}\ ,\qquad{K}^{\bar{a}}\equiv T_{A}{K}^{A}=d\bar{h}\bar{h}^{-1}\ ,

(4.25)

where
{J}
and
{K}
are the
currents that appear in the deformation term in (
4.20
).
Up to permutations, the non-zero components of the metric and
{\rm H}
-tensor of the
G\times G
model (
4.20
) are (cf. (
1.6
))

\displaystyle{\rm G}_{AB}={\rm G}_{\bar{A}\bar{B}}=\tfrac{{\tilde{k}}}{2}\,{\rm Tr}[T_{A}T_{B}]\ ,

(4.26)

\displaystyle{\rm H}_{ABC}=-i\tfrac{{\tilde{k}}}{2}\tfrac{\sqrt{1-{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}(1+2{\tilde{\lambda}})}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}})^{2}}f_{ABC}\ ,\qquad{\rm H}_{\bar{A}\bar{B}\bar{C}}=-i\tfrac{{\tilde{k}}}{2}f_{ABC}\ ,\qquad{\rm H}_{\bar{A}BC}=-i\tfrac{{\tilde{k}}}{2}\tfrac{{\tilde{\lambda}}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}}f_{ABC}\ .

(4.27)

Our aim is to compute the corresponding 2-loop
\beta
-function in (
1.9
).
Let us formally define the torsion as

T^{M}=\tfrac{1}{2}{{\rm H}^{M}}_{NP}E^{N}\wedge E^{P}
where the tangent space index is raised with the inverse of the metric (
4.26
).
Then, from the Cartan structure equation

dE^{M}+{\widehat{\omega}^{M}}{}_{N}\wedge E^{N}=T^{M}
, we obtain the torsionful spin connection

{{\widehat{\omega}}^{M}}{}_{N}={\widehat{\omega}^{M}}{}_{NP}E^{P}
with non-zero components

\displaystyle({\tilde{\lambda}}^{-1}+1){{\widehat{\omega}}^{A}}_{\ B}=\widehat{\omega}^{\bar{A}}{}_{\bar{B}}=if^{A}{}_{BC}\big(-\tfrac{{\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1-{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}e^{C}+e^{\bar{C}}\big)\ .

(4.28)

The non-zero components of the curvature,

{\widehat{R}^{M}}{}_{N}=d{\widehat{\omega}^{M}}{}_{N}+{\widehat{\omega}^{M}}{}_{P}\wedge{\widehat{\omega}^{P}}{}_{N}=\tfrac{1}{2}{\widehat{R}^{M}}{}_{NPQ}E^{P}\wedge E^{Q}
, are then found to be

\begin{split}({\tilde{\lambda}}^{-2}-1){\widehat{R}^{A}}{}_{BDE}=-\sqrt{{\tilde{\lambda}}^{-2}-1}{\widehat{R}^{A}}{}_{BD\bar{E}}={\widehat{R}^{A}}{}_{B\bar{D}\bar{E}}=-\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}})^{2}}f^{A}{}_{BC}f^{C}{}_{DE}\ .\end{split}

(4.29)

Substituting (
4.29
) into the 2-loop RG equation (
1.9
) in the
{\rm G}{\rm B}
scheme, one obtains

\big[\frac{d}{dt}(G_{mn}+B_{mn})\big]dx^{m}\otimes dx^{n}=\sum^{{\rm dim}\,G}_{A=\bar{A}=1}\Big[\frac{2c_{{}_{G}}{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}})^{2}}+\frac{4c_{{}_{G}}^{2}}{{\tilde{k}}}\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}^{4}(1-2{\tilde{\lambda}})}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}})^{5}(1-{\tilde{\lambda}})}\Big]\,{\rm Tr}[{J}\,\otimes\,{K}]\ .

(4.30)

Here
\otimes
indicates that the product is not symmetrized.
We conclude that only the
{\tilde{\lambda}}
-dependent term in (
4.20
) gets renormalized, i.e.
the 2-loop RG equation (
1.9
) is solved with
\xi_{m}=\lambda_{m}=0
and

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{\tilde{k}}=0\ ,\qquad\qquad\frac{d}{dt}{\tilde{\lambda}}=\frac{2c_{{}_{G}}}{{\tilde{k}}}\Big[\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}})^{2}}+\frac{2c_{{}_{G}}}{{\tilde{k}}}\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}^{4}(1-2{\tilde{\lambda}})}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}})^{5}(1-{\tilde{\lambda}})}\Big]\ .

(4.31)

The 1-loop term here agrees with previous results, both for the standard
\lambda
-model (eq. (
1.85
) with
{\kappa}=\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}-1}{{\tilde{\lambda}}+1}
)
[
195
,
153
]
and the “truncated”
G\times G
model (
4.20
)

[
190
,
192
]
).

The level
k={\tilde{k}}-2c_{{}_{G}}
is thus RG-invariant as it should be.
The fixed points of the RG flow are as expected:

{\tilde{\lambda}}=\infty
(i.e.
\lambda=0
), corresponding to the
G
WZW model with level
k
, and

{\tilde{\lambda}}=0
(i.e.
\lambda=\infty
) corresponding to the
G/G
cWZW model, or the
G
WZW model with level
-k
after integrating out the gauge field.

62
62

62

Taking

{\tilde{\lambda}}=1
(i.e.
\lambda=1
) with finite
k
(in contrast to the NAD limit
(
2.72
)) gives the trivial
G/G
gWZW model
in which one can fix a gauge so that the remaining degrees of
freedom correspond to the Cartan torus.

Expressing
{\tilde{k}}
and
{\tilde{\lambda}}
in (
4.19
) in terms of
k
and the coupling

{\kappa}
using (
4.3
), we find

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}k=0\ ,\qquad\qquad\frac{d}{dt}{\kappa}=\frac{c_{{}_{G}}}{4k}(1-{\kappa}^{2})^{2}\Big[1+\frac{c_{{}_{G}}(1-{\kappa})^{2}(1-10{\kappa}-3{\kappa}^{2})}{8k{\kappa}}\Big]\ .

(4.32)

Note that the 2-loop term in (
4.32
) is scheme-dependent: it can be changed by
redefining
{\kappa}
by a
1/k
term (or shifting
k
by a finite term): for example, it will depend on the choice of the parameter
{\rm q}
in
(
4.7
).
Even though
k
is not running, we effectively have a 2-coupling theory
(with
1/k
playing the role of a loop-counting parameter)
so only the 1-loop term in the
\beta
-function (
4.32
) is scheme-independent.

In a general scheme, the symmetry
k\to-k,\ \lambda\to\lambda^{-1}
(
1.80
)
of the 1-loop RG equation in (
4.31
),(
4.32
) is not manifest
at the 2-loop level.
To preserve it requires a particular formulation of the quantum theory, i.e. a specific definition of the couplings, or choice of scheme.
For example, if we redefine the parameters as

\displaystyle({\tilde{k}},{\tilde{\lambda}})\to({\rm k},\bar{\lambda})\ ,\qquad\qquad{\tilde{k}}={\rm k}+c_{{}_{G}}\ ,\qquad\quad{\tilde{\lambda}}=\bar{\lambda}\Big[1+c_{{}_{G}}{\rm k}^{-1}\frac{1-\bar{\lambda}}{1+\bar{\lambda}}\Big]\ ,

(4.33)

then the RG equation for
\bar{\lambda}
resulting from (
4.31
) is

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\bar{\lambda}=\frac{2c_{{}_{G}}}{{\rm k}}\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{2}}{(1+\bar{\lambda})^{2}}\Big[1-\frac{2c_{{}_{G}}}{{\rm k}}\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{2}}{(1+\bar{\lambda})^{2}}\frac{\bar{\lambda}+\bar{\lambda}^{-1}-1}{1-\bar{\lambda}^{2}}\Big]\ ,

(4.34)

which is invariant under
the following quantum version of (
1.80
)

\displaystyle{\rm k}\to-{\rm k}\ ,\qquad\qquad\bar{\lambda}\to\bar{\lambda}^{-1}\ .

(4.35)

Note that here
{\rm k}=k+c_{{}_{G}}
is the same as the shifted level of the WZW theory.
It is also worth observing that (
4.33
) is not the only redefinition that
restores the symmetry (
1.80
).
Indeed, even restricting to those that preserve the
existence of the fixed points and the NAD limit discussed below, there are many.

In the non-abelian dual (NAD) limit (
2.72
) (i.e.
{\kappa}=\tfrac{1}{4}{\rm h}k^{-1},\ k\to\infty
) when the
\lambda
-model (
4.15
) with unintegrated gauge field becomes the interpolating model (
1.52
) for non-abelian duality of the PCM,
the
\beta
-function (
4.32
) becomes

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{\rm h}={c_{{}_{G}}}+\tfrac{1}{2}{c_{{}_{G}}^{2}}{\rm h}^{-1}\ .

(4.36)

This result matches the standard expression (
1.20
) for the 2-loop
\beta
-function of the PCM on a simple group
G
(see, e.g.,
[
198
,
199
,
100
]
). The 2-loop term in (
4.36
) is scheme-independent as the NAD limit produces a single-coupling theory. Hence the scheme-dependent 2-loop term in the
\lambda
-model
\beta
-function (
4.32
) does still contain some invariant information, which passes a non-trivial test in this limit.

4.3
“Tripled”
\lambda
-model based on a symmetric space

Next let us consider the
\lambda
-model based on a symmetric space

G/H
(setting
P=P_{G/H}
in (
4.1
)),

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}=k\,\Big(\mathcal{L}_{G}(g)+{\rm Tr}\big[J_{+}A_{-}-A_{+}K_{-}+g^{-1}A_{+}gA_{-}-A_{+}\big(1+(\lambda^{-1}-1)P_{G/H}\big)A_{-}]\Big)\ ,\\
&g\in G\ ,\ \ A_{\pm}\in{\rm Lie}(G)\ .\end{aligned}

(4.37)

This model has a special subgroup
H\subset G
corresponding to its gauge symmetry, however we note that the 2d gauge field
A_{\pm}
is still valued in the algebra of the full group
G
.

We shall repeat the same steps as in the group case above, obtaining a similar tripled formulation:
redefining
(A_{+},A_{-})\to(h,\bar{h})
according to (
4.4
), using the Polyakov-Wiegmann identity (see footnote
58
),
including the contribution from the Jacobian (
4.7
), and
setting
\tilde{g}=h^{-1}g\bar{h}
and
{\hat{h}}=h^{-1}
, we now get

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{L}={}&{}k\mathcal{L}_{G}(\tilde{g})-(k+2c_{{}_{G}})\mathcal{L}_{G}({\hat{h}}\bar{h})+k(\lambda^{-1}-1){\rm Tr}[{\hat{h}}^{-1}\partial_{+}{\hat{h}}\,P_{G/H}\,\partial_{-}\bar{h}\bar{h}^{-1}]\\
{}&{}+2{\rm q}c_{{}_{G}}{\rm Tr}[{\hat{h}}^{-1}\partial_{+}{\hat{h}}\,\partial_{-}\bar{h}\bar{h}^{-1}]\ .\end{aligned}

(4.38)

Classically (i.e. for large
k
)
this model has the
expected
H
gauge symmetry

{\hat{h}}\to{\hat{h}}\,f\ ,\qquad\bar{h}\to f^{-1}\,\bar{h}\ ,\qquad\qquad f=f(\sigma^{+},\sigma^{-})\in H\ .

(4.39)

Since the term proportional to
q
in (
4.38
)
does not respect this gauge symmetry, then we
should make the
choice
{\rm q}=0
in the ambiguous Jacobian term in (
4.7
),
corresponding to a natural scheme
preserving the gauge symmetry (this is the same choice as in the gWZW case (
4.10
) above). Again applying the Polyakov-Wiegmann identity, we then obtain

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}&=k\mathcal{L}_{G}(\tilde{g})-(k+2c_{{}_{G}})\big[\mathcal{L}_{G}({\hat{h}})+\mathcal{L}_{G}(\bar{h})\big]\\
{}&{}\qquad\qquad\qquad+{\rm Tr}[{\hat{h}}^{-1}\partial_{+}{\hat{h}}\,\big(k+2c_{{}_{G}}+k(\lambda^{-1}-1)P_{G/H}\big)\,\partial_{-}\bar{h}\bar{h}^{-1}]\ .\end{split}

(4.40)

As in the group case, the first WZW term
k\mathcal{L}_{G}(\tilde{g})
decouples,
leaving the RG flow to be determined by the “truncated”
\sigma
-model,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}({\hat{h}},\bar{h})=-{\tilde{k}}\Big(\mathcal{L}_{G}({\hat{h}})+\mathcal{L}_{G}(\bar{h})-{\rm Tr}\Big[{\hat{h}}^{-1}\partial_{+}{\hat{h}}\,(P_{H}+{\tilde{\lambda}}\,P_{G/H})\,\partial_{-}\bar{h}\bar{h}^{-1}\Big]\Big)\ ,

(4.41)

\displaystyle\qquad{\tilde{k}}\equiv k+2c_{{}_{G}}\ ,\qquad{\tilde{\lambda}}\equiv\frac{k}{{\tilde{k}}}\lambda^{-1}+\frac{2c_{{}_{G}}}{{\tilde{k}}}\ .

(4.42)

This is a WZW model on
G\times G
, deformed a left-right current interaction term, now containing projectors
P_{H}
,
P_{G/H}
onto the subalgebra
{\rm Lie}(H)
its orthogonal complement.
The classical integrability of the model (
4.41
) (implying also the integrability of the
G/H

\lambda
-model)
was argued in
[
188
]
(example 4 there). It is also a special case of the integrable
G\times G/H
models
[
218
]
considered above in eqs. (
3.16
),(
3.19
),(
3.25
), with the couplings and fields related according to

\displaystyle(k,r,s,u,t,b)=(-{\tilde{k}},-{\tilde{k}},-{\tilde{k}},-{\tilde{k}},{\tilde{k}}{\tilde{\lambda}},{\tilde{k}}{\tilde{\lambda}})\ ,\qquad(g^{(1)},g^{(2)})=({\hat{h}},\bar{h}^{-1})\ .

(4.43)

The truncated model (
4.41
) is again automatically renormalizable due to manifest symmetries.
As well as the gauge symmetry (
4.39
) and discrete symmetries,
it is also invariant under the same chiral gauge transformations (
4.23
) as the group case. These symmetries protect the structure of (
4.41
), with
{\tilde{\lambda}}
and
{\tilde{k}}
being the only free parameters.

We shall explicitly demonstrate the renormalizability of the symmetric space
\lambda
-model (
4.37
) by computing the 2-loop
\beta
-function for the truncated model (
4.41
).
63
63

63

Like the group case above, the RG flow for the truncated model (
4.42
) is a special case of the that of the
G\times G/H
models in Chapter
3
(given explicitly in App.
D.2
). The results here are in agreement with those in App.
D.2
for the special values of the parameters in (
4.43
).

The computation runs analogously to the group case above, with the only difference being that one must correctly handle the gauge invariance.
We decompose the generators
\{T_{A}\}
of
G

into the generators
\{T_{\alpha}\}
of
H
and the remaining generators
\{T_{a}\}
chosen to be orthogonal to
{\rm Lie}{}(H)
under the trace, i.e. splitting the algebra index of
G
as
A=\{\alpha,a\}
(see Appendix
A
for conventions). Since
G/H
is a symmetric space then the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
grading of
{\rm Lie}(G)
implies
f_{\alpha\beta c}=f_{abc}=0
(i.e. the only non-zero structure constants are
f_{\alpha\beta\gamma}
and
f_{\alpha bc}
).
The tangent space index for
G\times G
now splits as
M=\{A;\bar{A}\}=\{\alpha,a;\bar{\alpha},\bar{a}\}
, with the unbarred and barred indices corresponding respectively to the two
G
-valued fields
\hat{h}
and
\bar{h}
.

Here we use the vielbein,

\displaystyle E^{M}=(e^{a},e^{\bar{a}},e^{\alpha},e^{\bar{\alpha}})=(\sqrt{1-{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{J}^{a},\,{K}^{\bar{a}}+{\tilde{\lambda}}{J}^{a},\,{J}^{\alpha}+{K}^{\bar{\alpha}},\,{J}^{\alpha}-{K}^{\bar{\alpha}})\ .

(4.44)

Up to permutations, the non-zero components of the metric and
{\rm H}
-tensor are

\displaystyle{\rm G}_{ab}={\rm G}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}}=\tfrac{\tilde{k}}{2}\,{\rm Tr}[T_{a}T_{b}]\ ,\qquad{\rm G}_{\alpha\beta}=\tfrac{\tilde{k}}{2}\,{\rm Tr}[T_{\alpha}T_{\beta}]\ ,

(4.45)

\displaystyle{\rm H}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=-i\tfrac{\tilde{k}}{2}f_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\ ,\qquad{\rm H}_{\alpha bc}=-i\tfrac{\tilde{k}}{2}f_{\alpha bc}\ ,\qquad{\rm H}_{\alpha\bar{b}\bar{c}}=-i\tfrac{\tilde{k}}{2}f_{\alpha bc}\ .

(4.46)

The
H
gauge symmetry of the model (
4.41
) leads to the
vanishing of all
\bar{\alpha}
-components of the metric and the
{\rm H}
-tensor.

As discussed in App.
D.2
,
there are various possible approaches to treat the
H
gauge symmetry.
Here we shall use the trick of lifting the degeneracy of the metric with a small parameter
\varepsilon
acting as a “regulator”,

\displaystyle{\rm G}_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}}=\varepsilon\,\tfrac{\tilde{k}}{2}\,{\rm Tr}[T_{\alpha}T_{\beta}]\ ,

(4.47)

computing the (torsionful) Riemann tensor, projecting out the
\bar{\alpha}
directions and setting the regulator
\varepsilon
to zero. This is a short-cut for a proper gauge-fixing procedure explained in footnote
92
.

The resulting torsionful curvature has
the non-zero components

\begin{split}({\tilde{\lambda}}^{-2}-1){\widehat{R}^{a}}{}_{bde}=-\sqrt{{\tilde{\lambda}}^{-2}-1}{\widehat{R}^{a}}{}_{bd\bar{e}}={\widehat{R}}^{a}{}_{b\bar{d}\bar{e}}=-f^{a}{}_{b\gamma}f^{\gamma}{}_{de}\ .\end{split}

(4.48)

Substituting this
into the RG equation (
1.9
), one obtains

{\big[\frac{d}{dt}({\rm G}_{mn}+{\rm B}_{mn})\big]}\,dx^{m}\otimes dx^{n}=\sum^{{\rm dim}\,G-{\rm dim}\,H}_{i=\bar{\imath}=1}\Big[c_{{}_{G}}{\tilde{\lambda}}+\frac{c_{{}_{G}}}{{\tilde{k}}}\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}(c_{{}_{H}}-(2c_{{}_{G}}-c_{{}_{H}}){\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})}{1-{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}\Big]\,{\rm Tr}[{J}\,\otimes\,P_{G/H}{K}]\ .

(4.49)

We conclude that only the
{\tilde{\lambda}}
-dependent term in (
4.41
) gets renormalized, i.e. the 2-loop RG equation (
1.9
) is solved with
\xi_{m}=\lambda_{m}=0
and

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{\tilde{k}}=0\ ,\qquad\qquad\frac{d}{dt}{\tilde{\lambda}}=\frac{c_{{}_{G}}{\tilde{\lambda}}}{{\tilde{k}}}\Big[1+\frac{1}{{\tilde{k}}}\frac{c_{{}_{H}}-(2c_{{}_{G}}-c_{{}_{H}}){\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{1-{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}\Big]\ .

(4.50)

The 1-loop term here agrees with the standard results for the symmetric space
\lambda
-model (eq. (
1.86
) with
{\kappa}=\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}-1}{{\tilde{\lambda}}+1}
)
[
195
,
153
]
.
The level
k
is RG-invariant, and, as expected,
{\tilde{\lambda}}=\infty
and
{\tilde{\lambda}}=0
are fixed points.
At the fixed point
{\tilde{\lambda}}=\infty
(
\lambda=0
) the
\lambda
-model reduces to the
G/H
gWZW model.
The other fixed point
{\tilde{\lambda}}=0
(
\lambda=-\tfrac{k}{2c_{{}_{G}}}
) is a quantum-corrected version of the 1-loop fixed point
\lambda=\infty
; this model is like a version of the
G/G
cWZW model with an additional
H
gauge symmetry, and after integrating out
A_{\pm}

it corresponds to the
G/H
gWZW model with level
-k
.

Expressing
{\tilde{k}}
and
{\tilde{\lambda}}
in (
4.42
) in terms of
k
and the coupling

{\kappa}
using (
4.3
), we find

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}k=0\ ,\qquad\qquad\frac{d}{dt}{\kappa}=\frac{c_{{}_{G}}}{2k}\Big[(1-{\kappa}^{2})+\frac{c_{{}_{G}}(1-{\kappa})^{2}(1+4{\kappa}-{\kappa}^{2})-c_{{}_{H}}(1-{\kappa}^{4})}{2k{\kappa}}\Big]\ .

(4.51)

As in the group space case (
4.32
), the 2-loop term in the

\beta
-function (
4.50
) is, in general, scheme-dependent.
Again, we find that the symmetry under
k\to-k
,
\lambda\to\lambda^{-1}
in (
1.80
),
present at the 1-loop order is absent in the 2-loop term of (
4.50
).
However, after introducing the shifted level
{\rm k}={\tilde{k}}-c_{{}_{G}}=k+c_{{}_{G}}
as in (
4.33
), the 2-loop RG equation for
{\tilde{\lambda}}
(
4.50
) becomes

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{\tilde{\lambda}}=\frac{c_{{}_{G}}{\tilde{\lambda}}}{{\rm k}}\Big[1-\frac{1}{{\rm k}}(c_{{}_{G}}-c_{{}_{H}})\frac{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{1-{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}\Big]\ ,

(4.52)

which is manifestly invariant under
{\rm k}\to-{\rm k}
,
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to{\tilde{\lambda}}^{-1}
, i.e.
a quantum version of the symmetry (
1.80
) of the original couplings (cf. (
4.35
)).

In the NAD limit (
2.72
), when the extended-space
\lambda
-model becomes the interpolating model (
1.52
) for NAD of the symmetric space, we get from (
4.51
)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{\rm h}=2{c_{{}_{G}}}+4{c_{{}_{G}}(c_{{}_{G}}-c_{{}_{H}})}{{\rm h}}^{-1}\ .

(4.53)

As verified in Appendix B of
[
3
]
, equation (
4.53
) is the correct 2-loop
\beta
-function for the
G/H
symmetric space
\sigma
-model (matching eq. (
1.32
) above and, in particular cases, the results in
[
200
,
199
]
). Like the group case above, the 2-loop coefficient here is also scheme-independent in this single-coupling limit, providing a non-trivial, scheme-invariant test of the
\lambda
-model’s 2-loop
\beta
-function.

4.4
Low-dimensional examples:
\lambda
-models for
SU(2)
and

SU(2)/U(1)

Let us study the simplest case of the truncated model (
4.20
) in the group case with
G=SU(2)
.
Parametrizing

{\hat{h}}=\exp(i\phi\sigma_{3})\exp(i\theta\sigma_{1})\exp(i\psi\sigma_{3})\ ,\qquad\quad\bar{h}=\exp(i\bar{\phi}\sigma_{3})\exp(i\bar{\theta}\sigma_{1})\exp(i\bar{\psi}\sigma_{3})\ ,

(4.54)

where
\sigma_{A}
are the standard Pauli matrices, we find the following 6-dimensional metric and
{\rm B}
-field (with
{\rm B}=\tfrac{1}{2}{\rm B}_{mn}dx^{m}\wedge dx^{n}
)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad ds^{2}=(ds^{2})_{G\times G}+(ds^{2})_{\rm int}\ ,\qquad\quad{\rm B}={\rm B}_{G\times G}+{\rm B}_{\rm int}\ ,

(4.55)

\displaystyle(ds^{2})_{G\times G}=-{\tilde{k}}(d\theta^{2}+d\phi^{2}+d\psi^{2}+2\cos 2\theta d\phi d\psi+d\bar{\theta}^{2}+d\bar{\phi}^{2}+d\bar{\psi}^{2}+2\cos 2\bar{\theta}d\bar{\phi}d\bar{\psi})\ ,

\displaystyle{\rm B}_{G\times G}=-{\tilde{k}}(\cos 2\theta d\phi\wedge d\psi+\cos 2\bar{\theta}d\bar{\phi}\wedge d\bar{\psi})\ ,

\displaystyle(ds^{2})_{\rm int}=-2{\tilde{k}}{\tilde{\lambda}}\big[\cos 2(\psi+\bar{\phi})(d\theta d\bar{\theta}-\sin 2\theta\sin 2\bar{\theta}d\phi d\bar{\psi})+(d\psi+\cos 2\theta d\phi)(d\bar{\phi}+\cos 2\bar{\theta}d\bar{\psi})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sin 2(\psi+\bar{\phi})(\sin 2\theta d\phi d\bar{\theta}+\sin 2\bar{\theta}d\theta d\bar{\psi})\big]\ ,

\displaystyle{\rm B}_{\rm int}=-{\tilde{k}}{\tilde{\lambda}}\big[\cos 2(\psi+\bar{\phi})(d\theta\wedge d\bar{\theta}-\sin 2\theta\sin 2\bar{\theta}d\phi\wedge d\bar{\psi})+(d\psi+\cos 2\theta d\phi)\wedge(d\bar{\phi}+\cos 2\bar{\theta}d\bar{\psi})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sin 2(\psi+\bar{\phi})(\sin 2\theta d\phi\wedge d\bar{\theta}+\sin 2\bar{\theta}d\theta\wedge d\bar{\psi})\big]\ .

Here the “
G\times G
” terms come from the WZW models and the “int” terms come from the current-current interactions between them.
One can check explicitly that this metric and
{\rm B}
-field solve the 2-loop RG equation (
1.9
) with vanishing
\xi^{m}
and
\lambda_{m}
, and the couplings running as in (
4.31
) (with the dual Coxeter number
c_{{}_{SU(2)}}=2
).

Next, let us consider the truncated model (
4.41
) obtained from the
\lambda
-model based on the symmetric space
G/H=SU(2)/U(1)
.
Using the parametrization for
{\hat{h}}
and
\bar{h}
given in eq. (
4.54
),
we find that the corresponding
target space background depends on
\psi
and
\bar{\phi}
only
through the combination
\chi=\psi+\bar{\phi}
, which is a manifestation of the
U(1)
gauge symmetry.
The resulting 5-dimensional metric and
{\rm B}
-field are

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad ds^{2}=(ds^{2})_{G\times G/H}+(ds^{2})_{\rm int}\ ,\qquad\qquad{\rm B}={\rm B}_{G\times G/H}+{\rm B}_{\rm int}\ ,

(4.56)

\displaystyle(ds^{2})_{G\times G/H}=-{\tilde{k}}(d\theta^{2}+d\phi^{2}+d\bar{\theta}^{2}+d\bar{\psi}^{2})\ ,\qquad{\rm B}_{G\times G/H}=0\ ,

\displaystyle(ds^{2})_{\rm int}=-2{\tilde{k}}{\tilde{\lambda}}\big[\cos 2\chi(d\theta d\bar{\theta}-\sin 2\theta\sin 2\bar{\theta}d\phi d\bar{\psi})+\sin 2\chi(\sin 2\theta d\phi d\bar{\theta}+\sin 2\bar{\theta}d\theta d\bar{\psi})\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+{\tilde{k}}d\chi^{2}-2{\tilde{k}}(d\chi+\cos 2\theta d\phi)(d\chi+\cos 2\bar{\theta}d\bar{\psi})\ ,

\displaystyle{\rm B}_{\rm int}=-{\tilde{k}}{\tilde{\lambda}}\big[\cos 2\chi(d\theta\wedge d\bar{\theta}-\sin 2\theta\sin 2\bar{\theta}d\phi\wedge d\bar{\psi})+\sin 2\chi(\sin 2\theta d\phi\wedge d\bar{\theta}+\sin 2\bar{\theta}d\theta\wedge d\bar{\psi})\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-{\tilde{k}}(d\chi+\cos 2\theta d\phi)\wedge(d\chi+\cos 2\bar{\theta}d\bar{\psi})\ .

Again one can check explicitly that this metric and
{\rm B}
-field solve the 2-loop RG equation
(
1.9
) with vanishing
\xi^{m}
and
\lambda_{m}
, and the couplings running as in (
4.50
)
(with the dual Coxeter numbers
c_{{}_{SU(2)}}=2
and
c_{{}_{U(1)}}=0
).

4.5
“Tripled” formulations of other models

In principle the trick of rewriting 2d gauge fields in terms of group-valued scalars can be applied to any theory with 2d gauge fields. In this section, containing unpublished work, we shall construct analogous “tripled” or “extended” formulations of two other classes of
\sigma
-models.

4.5.1
G^{N}

\lambda
-models

First let us consider a generalization of the
\lambda
-model to
N
copies of the group
G
, which was recently obtained from affine Gaudin models
[
65
]
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=k_{i}\,\mathcal{L}_{G/G}(g^{(i)},A^{(i)})-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\gamma_{ij}\,{\rm Tr}[A^{(i)}_{+}A^{(j)}_{-}]\ ,\qquad\qquad i=1,\ldots n\ .

(4.57)

Here
k_{i}
are the WZ levels and
\gamma_{ij}
is
2\times 2
matrix of coupling constants. Integrating out the quadratic gauge fields
A_{\pm}^{(i)}
yields a
\sigma
-model with scalar fields
g^{(i)}\in G
.

This may be viewed as an example of Tseytlin’s “universal class” model (
1.77
) adapted to
G^{N}
, with the constant operator
Q
chosen to preserve the global
G
symmetry
(g^{(i)},A^{(i)})\to(ug^{(i)}u^{-1},uA^{(i)}u^{-1})
,
u\in G
. Like the
G^{N}
models discussed in Chapter
3
, the theory (
4.57
) is classically integrable only for special values of the coupling constants
(k_{i},\gamma_{ij})
, parametrized algebraically in terms of the twist function in
[
65
]
. Although we shall not work out the details here, this parametrization may be recast as a set of polynomial equations in
(k_{i},\gamma_{ij})
(cf. the equation (
3.6
) for undeformed
G\times G
models).

Like standard
\lambda
-models, the
G^{N}

\lambda
-model (
4.57
) is a deformation of a WZW model (when
\gamma_{ij}
is taken to infinity while being non-degenerate)
and becomes in a particular limit,
k_{i}\to\infty
,
g^{(i)}=e^{-\tfrac{v^{(i)}}{2k_{i}}}
, the non-abelian dual of a particular
G^{N}
model (
3.2
) in its
(G_{L})^{N}
symmetry,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,{\rm Tr}[\gamma_{ij}A^{(i)}_{+}A^{(j)}_{-}-v^{(i)}F_{+-}(A^{(i)})]\ ,\qquad F_{+-}(A)\equiv\partial_{+}A_{-}-\partial_{-}A_{+}+[A_{+},A_{-}]\ .

(4.58)

Our observation is that the tripled formulation of the
\lambda
-model generalizes very easily to this
G^{N}
case. Let us perform the same change of variables
(A^{(i)}_{+},A^{(i)}_{-})\to(h^{(i)},\bar{h}^{(i)})
on each copy of the group, where
A^{(i)}_{+}=h^{(i)}\partial_{+}\big(h^{(i)}\big)^{-1}
,
A^{(i)}_{-}=\bar{h}^{(i)}\partial_{-}\big(\bar{h}^{(i)}\big)^{-1}
(cf. eq. (
4.4
)). Including the Jacobian (
4.7
) for this transformation (and picking again
{\rm q}=-1
), the resulting theory is a “tripled”
\sigma
-model with fields
(g^{(i)},h^{(i)},\bar{h}^{(i)})\in G^{3N}
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}=k_{i}\,\mathcal{L}_{G}(\tilde{g}^{(i)})+\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\hat{h}^{(i)},\bar{h}^{(i)})\ ,\qquad\qquad\tilde{g}^{(i)}\equiv\big(h^{(i)}\big)^{-1}g^{(i)}\bar{h}^{(i)}\ ,\quad\hat{h}^{(i)}\equiv\big(h^{(i)}\big)\ ,\\
&\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=-(k_{i}+2c_{{}_{G}})\big[\mathcal{L}_{G}(\hat{h}^{(i)})+\mathcal{L}_{G}(\bar{h}^{(i)})\big]+{\rm Tr}\big[(k_{i}\delta_{ij}-\tfrac{1}{2}\gamma_{ij})\,J_{+}^{(i)}\,\bar{K}_{-}^{(j)}\big]\ ,\\
&\qquad J_{+}^{(i)}\equiv\big(\hat{h}^{(i)}\big)^{-1}\partial_{+}\hat{h}^{(i)}\ ,\qquad\bar{K}_{-}^{(i)}\equiv\partial_{+}\bar{h}^{(i)}\big(\bar{h}^{(i)}\big)^{-1}\ .\end{aligned}

(4.59)

This is a particular case of the undeformed
G^{M}
model (
3.2
) with
M=3N
(after a redefinition
\bar{h}^{(i)}\to\big(\bar{h}^{(i)}\big)^{-1}
).

As in the
\lambda
-model considered above, the first WZW terms
k_{i}\,\mathcal{L}_{G}(\tilde{g}^{(i)})
in (
4.59
) decouple, leaving the RG flow to be determined by the “truncated” Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
, which is a
\sigma
-model with fields
(h^{(i)},\bar{h}^{(i)})\in G^{2N}
. This truncated theory is also a particular case of the undeformed
G^{M}
model (
3.2
) with
M=2N
.

Hence we have produced a quantum equivalence between (i) the
\lambda
-deformation (
4.57
) based on
G^{N}
models and (ii) certain
G^{M}
models with
M=2N
(plus
N
decoupled WZW models). In effect, moving from the
G^{N}
model to the related
\lambda
-deformation is equivalent to replacing
N\to 2N
, restricting to particular class of the
G^{2N}
model’s couplings, and adding
N
decoupled WZW models.

Let us make some observations. As mentioned above, the
G^{N}

\lambda
-model is only integrable for special values of the couplings solving polynomial equations. This is indeed consistent with the equivalence found here, since the same is true for the undeformed
G^{M}
models of which the tripled formulation (
4.59
) is a special case. Hence one should be able to understand the integrability conditions for
G^{N}

\lambda
-models in terms of those of undeformed
G^{M}
models; in this respect the
\lambda
-deformation does not introduce any new elements. It would be interesting to understand the relation between these models at the level of affine Gaudin models and the twist functions.

Renormalizability of the
G^{N}

\lambda
-model (
4.57
) does not seem obvious based alone on its manifest
G
symmetry, which is not sufficient to protect the form of the action. However, in the tripled formulation (
4.59
), there is again a decoupling of some fields
\tilde{g}^{i}
and the remaining truncated theory
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is protected by
G(\xi^{-})^{N}\times G(\xi^{+})^{N}\times G
chiral gauge symmetry,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &(\hat{h}^{(i)},\ \bar{h}^{(i)})\to(u^{(i)}(\xi^{-})\,\hat{h}^{(i)}\,w,\ w^{-1}\,\bar{h}^{(i)}\,v^{(i)}(\xi^{+}))\ ,\\
&u^{(i)}(\xi^{-})\in G(\xi^{-})\ ,\quad v^{(i)}(\xi^{+})\in G(\xi^{+})\ ,\quad w\in G\ ,\end{aligned}

(4.60)

which uniquely fixes the theory up to the parameters
(k_{i},\gamma_{ij})
. Hence it will be renormalizable to all orders with just
\gamma_{ij}
running (while the WZ levels do not run for general reasons). Since the original and tripled formulations are equivalent at the quantum level then we have proved the renormalizability of the
G^{N}

\lambda
-model (
4.57
) with unintegrated gauge field to all orders.

So far we have established the renormalizability of the
G^{N}

\lambda
-model (
4.57
) before imposing the integrability condition. We expect the “integrable surface” in the space of couplings
(k_{i},\gamma_{ij})
to be a stable subspace under the RG flow (cf. the
G\times G
and
G\times G/H
models studied in Chapter
3
). It would be interesting to check this fact and to see whether the integrability conditions require quantum corrections to remain stable beyond 1-loop. The tripled formulation (
4.59
) is the natural arena to study these questions since it is already RG-stable with a finite set of couplings before imposing the integrability condition.

4.5.2
G\times G/H
models

Now let us consider the
H
-gauge-invariant
G\times G/H
models considered in Section
3.2
, with Lagrangian,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\rho_{ij}\,{\rm Tr}[P^{(i)}_{+}P^{(j)}_{-}]-\tfrac{1}{2}\,r_{ij}\,{\rm Tr}[{B}^{(i)}_{+}{B}^{(j)}_{-}]+k_{i}\,\mathcal{L}_{{}_{\rm WZ}}(g^{(i)})\ ,\\
&k_{1}=-k_{2}\equiv k\ ,\qquad r_{ij}=\begin{pmatrix}r&-r-k\\
-r+k&r\end{pmatrix}\ ,\\
&P^{(i)}=P_{{G/H}}J^{(i)}\ ,\qquad{B}^{(i)}=P_{H}J^{(i)}\ ,\ \ \qquad J^{(i)}={g^{(i)}}^{-1}dg^{(i)}\ .\end{aligned}

(4.61)

First let us re-write the theory in terms of a 2d gauge field by starting with an ungauged (
G\times G
)-type model (here
R_{ij}
are arbitrary constants and
k_{1}=-k_{2}=-k
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\rho_{ij}\,{\rm Tr}[P^{(i)}_{+}P^{(j)}_{-}]-\tfrac{1}{2}\,R_{ij}\,{\rm Tr}[{B}^{(i)}_{+}{B}^{(j)}_{-}]+k\,\big(\mathcal{L}_{{}_{\rm WZ}}(g^{(1)})-\mathcal{L}_{{}_{\rm WZ}}(g^{(2)})\big)\ ,

(4.62)

and gauging the
H
global symmetry
(g^{(1)},g^{(2)})\to(g^{(1)}h,g^{(2)}h)
,
h\in H
by the same gauging procedure discussed for coset spaces in Section
1.4.3
. Promoting
h\to h(\xi)
to a local parameter, the Lagrangian transforms as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}\to-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\rho_{ij}\,{\rm Tr}[P^{(i)}_{+}P^{(j)}_{-}]+k\,\big(\mathcal{L}_{{}_{\rm WZ}}(g^{(1)})-\mathcal{L}_{{}_{\rm WZ}}(g^{(2)})\big)\\
&\qquad-\tfrac{1}{2}\,{\rm Tr}[R_{ij}\,{B}^{(i)}_{+}{B}^{(j)}_{-}+\partial_{+}hh^{-1}(\alpha\,B_{-}^{(1)}+\beta\,B_{-}^{(2)})\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\partial_{-}hh^{-1}(\gamma\,B_{+}^{(1)}+\delta\,B_{+}^{(2)})+\varepsilon\,\partial_{+}hh^{-1}\,\partial_{-}hh^{-1}],\\
&\alpha=R_{11}+R_{21}-k\ ,\quad\beta=R_{12}+R_{22}+k\ ,\\
&\gamma=R_{11}+R_{12}+k\ ,\quad\delta=R_{21}+R_{22}-k\ ,\quad\varepsilon=R_{11}+R_{12}+R_{21}+R_{22}\ .\end{aligned}

(4.63)

Now promoting
\partial_{+}hh^{-1}\to-A_{\pm}
in the transformed Lagrangian (
4.63
), the resulting theory is gauge invariant (under
g\to h^{-1}gh
,
A_{\pm}\to h^{-1}A_{\pm}h+h^{-1}\partial_{\pm}h
,
h(\xi)\in H
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(g,A)={}

\displaystyle{}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\rho_{ij}\,{\rm Tr}[P^{(i)}_{+}P^{(j)}_{-}]+k\,\big(\mathcal{L}_{{}_{\rm WZ}}(g^{(1)})-\mathcal{L}_{{}_{\rm WZ}}(g^{(2)})\big)

(4.64)

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}\,{\rm Tr}[R_{ij}\,{B}^{(i)}_{+}{B}^{(j)}_{-}-A_{+}(\alpha\,B_{-}^{(1)}+\beta\,B_{-}^{(2)})-A_{-}(\gamma\,B_{+}^{(1)}+\delta\,B_{+}^{(2)})+\varepsilon\,A_{+}A_{-}]\ .

Upon integrating out the 2d gauge field
A_{\pm}
, one indeed obtains the desired
G\times G/H
model (
4.61
) with the following identification of parameters

\displaystyle r=\frac{k^{2}+k(R_{12}-R_{21})+R_{11}R_{22}-R_{12}R_{21}}{R_{11}+R_{12}+R_{21}+R_{22}}\ ,\qquad k=k\ .

(4.65)

Hence the model (
4.64
) with the gauge field is equivalent to the
G\times G/H
model (
4.61
) and, moreover, only one combination of the couplings
R_{ij}
appears after integrating out
A_{\pm}
. This means one is free to fix any 3 components (or combinations of components) of
R_{ij}
to arbitrary values, as long as
R_{11}+R_{12}+R_{21}+R_{22}\neq 0
so that (
4.65
) does not diverge.

For general
R_{ij}
we may again change variables
(A_{+},A_{-})\to(h,\bar{h})
according to (
4.4
) (this is like replacing
\partial_{-}hh^{-1}\to\partial_{-}\bar{h}\bar{h}^{-1}
in (
4.63
)). The resulting theory is a
\sigma
-model with an enlarged configuration space
(g^{(1)},g^{(2)},h,\bar{h})\in G\times G\times H\times H
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{L}(g^{(1)},g^{(2)},h,\bar{h})={}&{}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\rho_{ij}\,{\rm Tr}[P^{(i)}_{+}P^{(j)}_{-}]+k\,\big(\mathcal{L}_{{}_{\rm WZ}}(g^{(1)})-\mathcal{L}_{{}_{\rm WZ}}(g^{(2)})\big)\\
&-\tfrac{1}{2}\,{\rm Tr}[R_{ij}\,{B}^{(i)}_{+}{B}^{(j)}_{-}+\partial_{+}hh^{-1}(\alpha\,B_{-}^{(1)}+\beta\,B_{-}^{(2)})\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\partial_{-}\bar{h}\bar{h}^{-1}(\gamma\,B_{+}^{(1)}+\delta\,B_{+}^{(2)})+\varepsilon\,\partial_{+}hh^{-1}\partial_{-}\bar{h}\bar{h}^{-1}]\ .\end{aligned}

(4.66)

One may fix the gauge symmetry as, e.g.,
\bar{h}=1
(or
A_{-}=0
) to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}(g^{(1)},g^{(2)},h)=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\rho_{ij}\,{\rm Tr}[P^{(i)}_{+}P^{(j)}_{-}]+k\,\big(\mathcal{L}_{{}_{\rm WZ}}(g^{(1)})-\mathcal{L}_{{}_{\rm WZ}}(g^{(2)})\big)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-\tfrac{1}{2}\,{\rm Tr}[R_{ij}\,{B}^{(i)}_{+}{B}^{(j)}_{-}+\partial_{+}hh^{-1}(\alpha\,B_{-}^{(1)}+\beta\,B_{-}^{(2)})]\ ,\\
&\alpha=R_{11}+R_{21}-k\ ,\qquad\beta=R_{12}+R_{22}+k\ .\end{aligned}

(4.67)

The resulting gauge-fixed model (
4.67
) is a
\sigma
-model with configuration space
(g^{(1)},g^{(2)},h)\in G\times G\times H
. It may be described as a particular “squashed”
G\times G\times H
model, since the
G\times G
sector includes dependence on the projectors
P_{H}
and
P_{G/H}
.

One simple example of (
4.67
) obtained from the choice

\displaystyle R_{ij}=\begin{pmatrix}r&0\\
-r+k&0\end{pmatrix}\ ,

(4.68)

is given by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{L}(g^{(1)},g^{(2)},h)={}&{}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\rho_{ij}\,{\rm Tr}[P^{(i)}_{+}P^{(j)}_{-}]+k\,\big(\mathcal{L}_{{}_{\rm WZ}}(g^{(1)})-\mathcal{L}_{{}_{\rm WZ}}(g^{(2)})\big)\\
&-\tfrac{1}{2}\,{\rm Tr}[(r{B}^{(1)}_{+}+(k-r){B}^{(2)}_{+}){B}^{(1)}_{-}+k\,\partial_{+}hh^{-1}B_{-}^{(2)}]\ .\end{aligned}

(4.69)

Another natural choice consistent with (
4.65
) is

\displaystyle R_{ij}={\epsilon}\,\rho_{ij}\ ,\qquad r=\frac{k^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}+k(R_{12}-R_{21})+\varepsilon(R_{11}R_{22}-R_{12}R_{21})}{R_{11}+R_{12}+R_{21}+R_{22}}\ .

(4.70)

Then (
4.67
) becomes

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}(g^{(1)},g^{(2)},h)=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\rho_{ij}\,{\rm Tr}[\varepsilon B^{(i)}_{+}B^{(j)}+P^{(i)}_{+}P^{(j)}_{-}]+k\,\big(\mathcal{L}_{{}_{\rm WZ}}(g^{(1)})-\mathcal{L}_{{}_{\rm WZ}}(g^{(2)})\big)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-\tfrac{1}{2}\,{\rm Tr}[\partial_{+}hh^{-1}\,\big(\alpha\,B_{-}^{(1)}+\beta\,B_{-}^{(2)}\big)]\ ,\\
&\alpha=\varepsilon(\rho_{11}+\rho_{21})-k\ ,\qquad\beta=\varepsilon(\rho_{12}+\rho_{22})+k\ .\end{aligned}

(4.71)

These extended-space reformulations of the
G\times G/H
models will inherit the same classical integrability and RG flow properties as the original theory (modulo the inclusion of a Jacobian of the same form as (
4.7
)). Hence we learn that the extended
G\times G\times H
models (
4.67
) (and the particular cases (
4.69
) and (
4.71
)) should also be integrable when the couplings
r,k,\rho_{ij}
satisfy the integrability conditions (
3.25
) of the
G\times G/H
model.
Speculatively, one might hope that, after imposing the integrability conditions, there could be a decoupling of some fields (as for the
\lambda
-models studied above), which may leave the remaining theory manifestly renormalizable due to its global symmetries. Such a phenomenon would be interesting as (i) it could provide another origin for the integrability conditions (
3.25
) of the
G\times G/H
model and (ii) in such a reformulation, the
G\times G/H
model’s integrability condition would become automatically stable at higher-loop orders (cf. eq. (
3.34
) in the standard formulation).

4.6
Discussion

Formulating the
\lambda
-model on an extended
(
G\times G\times G
)
configuration space
“linearizes” the RG flow, i.e. makes its renormalizability manifest without the need
for extra local counterterms apart from the running of the coupling
\lambda
.
This implies that the
\lambda
-model with unintegrated gauge field is renormalizable to all orders.

As well as eliminating the need for quantum corrections to remain renormalizable at higher-loop orders, the tripled formulation also explains the
\lambda
-model’s renormalizability already at the 1-loop order. Whereas the standard formulation (
4.1
) is apparently protected under the RG flow by
hidden
symmetries, the tripled formulation is protected by
manifest
symmetries.

The decoupling of one of the WZW models
k\mathcal{L}_{G}(\tilde{g})
in (
4.17
) and (
4.40
) appears to be the special property that renders the manifest global symmetries sufficient for renormalizability of the remaining “truncated” models. This property should be associated with integrability, since it would be lost under generic deformations of the
\lambda
-model (
4.1
) preserving its manifest symmetries but breaking integrability. It would be interesting in future to understand the precise relationship between integrability and this special decoupling in the tripled formulation.

In the non-abelian dual limit (
2.72
), the resulting scheme-invariant 2-loop
\beta
-functions matched the known
\beta
-functions for the PCM and symmetric space. One may regard this tripled computation as a particular scheme for the renormalization of the interpolating models (
1.52
) for non-abelian duality (obtained from the tripled
\lambda
-model in this limit). One might worry that the NAD limit could be too singular to commute with the RG flow (e.g., the decoupling of the
\mathcal{L}_{G}(\tilde{g})
term in (
4.17
) breaks down in that limit),
but this is not likely since one can independently argue the renormalizability of the interpolating models (
1.52
)
through their relation to the original PCM/symmetric space models
by
an integral over the linear Lagrange multiplier
v
.
This integral is not summing any loops as
v
only appears linearly, so the classical result
\delta(F_{+-}(A))
is expected to be consistent with the RG flow, without needing any extra counterterms.
64
64

64

I thank A.A. Tseytlin for a discussion of this point.
It would be interesting in future to check this fact directly, e.g. computing the RG flow for a “tripled” formulation of the interpolating models for NAD.

If one regards the interpolating models, or the corresponding tripled
\sigma
-models, as particular formulations of the corresponding NAD theories at the quantum level,
then
it is natural to conclude that non-abelian duality commutes
with the RG flow beyond the 1-loop order on arbitrary groups
G
and symmetric spaces
G/H
,
with the original and NAD models having the same 2-loop
\beta
-function. This is in line with previous 1-loop conclusions
[
154
,
155
]
and resolves related confusions at the 2-loop level
[
140
,
141
,
142
,
143
]
.

Hence, for (i) the
\lambda
-models with unintegrated gauge field and (ii) the interpolating models for NAD,
the non-trivial corrections that were required in the standard configuration space in Chapter
2
should be
understood as finite counterterms resulting from the integral over the 2d gauge field appearing quadratically in the action.
It remains to understand the general structure of those counterterms, or equivalently the
correct prescription for the 2d gauge field integral consistent with integrability
(generalizing (
2.42
), which was sufficient only in the simple
SU(2)\over U(1)
example).

The 2-loop
\beta
-functions (
4.32
) and (
4.51
) obtained through the tripled formulation are consistent with those found for
particular examples of the
\lambda
-model in the standard formulation in eqs. (
2.45
) and (
2.57
), modulo a redefinition of the couplings
k,\lambda
, i.e. scheme change (see
[
3
]
for the precise redefinitions that relate them). In particular this means that the
scheme-invariant 1-loop terms match exactly; moreover the 2-loop terms also match in the single-coupling NAD limit when they become scheme-invariant.

A similar “tripled” approach may also be useful
for clarifying the higher-loop deformation in abelian T-duality.
For example, consider the metric
ds^{2}=dy^{2}+a(y)dx^{2}
and its classical dual

\widetilde{ds^{2}}=dy^{2}+a(y)^{-1}d\tilde{x}^{2}
,

with the interpolating model given by

\displaystyle-\mathcal{L}=(\partial_{\mu}y)^{2}+a(y)(p_{\mu})^{2}+\tilde{x}\,\varepsilon^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}p_{\nu}\ ,

(4.72)

such that
y
is a spectator coordinate (cf. eq. (
1.48
)).
If we integrate out
\tilde{x}
to give
p_{\mu}=\partial_{\mu}x
, we recover the
original model for
x
.
If we integrate out
p_{\mu}
, we find the T-dual model for
\tilde{x}
.
If instead we set
p_{1}=\partial_{1}x
and integrate out
p_{0}
we get the
“doubled” model of
[
205
]
for
x
and
\tilde{x}
(equivalent
to the “axial” gauge choice in the Appendix of
[
206
]
).
The “tripled” model for (
x,\tilde{x},\bar{x}
), analogous to the ones discussed above, is obtained by setting
p_{\mu}=\partial_{\mu}x+\varepsilon_{\mu\nu}\partial^{\nu}\bar{x}
:

\displaystyle-\mathcal{L}=(\partial_{\mu}y)^{2}+a(y)[(\partial_{\mu}x)^{2}-(\partial_{\mu}\bar{x})^{2}+2\varepsilon^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}x\partial_{\nu}\bar{x}]+\partial_{\mu}\tilde{x}\partial^{\mu}\bar{x}\ .

(4.73)

This may be interpreted as a
\sigma
-model on 4-dimensional target space with pp-wave metric and
{\rm B}
-field.

We also considered similar “tripled” or “extended” formulations for other models.
We found that the tripled formulation of the
\lambda
-model generalizes easily to
\lambda
-models based on
G^{N}
. In this tripled formulation, it is clear that the
G^{N}

\lambda
-models are renormalizable to all orders due to manifest symmetries (before imposing the integrability condition).
The resulting tripled
\sigma
-models are particular class of
G^{3N}
models, or
G^{2N}
models after removing decoupled WZW models.
Hence in this case we find a correspondence between the integrability and RG flow properties of
G^{N}

\lambda
-models and certain undeformed
G^{2N}
models.

We reformulated the
G\times G/H
models studied in Chapter
3
in terms of a gauge field and found a 3-parameter family of extended formulations (3 of the components of
R_{ij}
, with the 4th being fixed by the relation (
4.65
)). The resulting
\sigma
-model has configuration space
G\times G\times H\times H
, or
G\times G\times H
after fixing the
H
gauge symmetry. Hence we find a correspondence between the integrability and RG properties of
G\times G/H
models and certain “squashed”
G\times G\times H
models. It would be interesting to study the RG flow for both
G^{N}

\lambda
-models and
G\times G/H
models in these new, extended formulations.

A useful extension of the tripled formulation would be to the
\eta
-model.
Up to analytic continuation, the
\eta
-model and
\lambda
-model are related by limits and T-duality
[
151
,
159
]

or by Poisson-Lie duality
[
151
,
158
,
156
,
159
]
,
so it seems plausible that the tripled formulation of the
\lambda
-model could lead to a similar approach for the
\eta
-model.
This could allow a generalizaton of our results for the
SU(2)\over U(1)

\eta
-model in Section
2.2
to general groups and symmetric spaces.

Another obvious generalization would be to
\sigma
-models on supergroups and
supercosets (see, e.g.,

[
214
,
215
,
172
,
153
]
), and to integrable-deformed superstring
\sigma
-models.
See Section
6.4
for further discussion of this possibility.

Chapter 5
Sigma models with local couplings

There is a remarkable link between the
integrability of 2d
\sigma
-models and their stability under RG flow, i.e. their
renormalizability with finitely many running couplings.
However, it
remains rather mysterious how the integrability translates into simple
RG behaviour.

In this chapter we shall present a new and different link between
classical integrability and the RG flow (based on the paper
[
4
]
and closely following the presentation there).
Consider a familiar example of an integrable 2d
\sigma
-model, the
principal chiral model (PCM). Suppose we
formally replace its overall coupling
h

by a function of 2d time
\tau
,
obtaining
the
Lagrangian

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}=-\tfrac{1}{2}h(\tau)\,{\rm Tr}[J_{+}J_{-}]\ .

(5.1)

The resulting time-dependent theory is not Lorentz-invariant but one may wonder if
some notion of classical integrability still applies even for a non-constant
h(\tau)
.
As we shall see below,
this is indeed the case
provided
the function
h(\tau)
is special:
the same as the solution of the 1-loop RG equation of the standard PCM,
i.e.
h(\tau)\sim\tau
.

More generally, starting with a Lorentz-invariant
2d theory
that is classically integrable (i.e. admitting a Lax connection),
one may formally promote
its coupling constants
h_{\alpha}
to functions
h_{\alpha}(\tau)
depending on 2d time.
We address the question of which functions
h_{\alpha}(\tau)

allow the integrability to survive in the resulting time-dependent theory, in the sense that the resulting equations of motion still
admit a Lax representation.
We shall suggest a certain natural ansatz for the corresponding Lax connection
generalizing that of the time-independent theory and
will find that the unique functions
h_{\alpha}(\tau)
preserving the integrability under this ansatz are the ones solving the 1-loop RG equations of the original theory,

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}h_{\alpha}=\beta_{\alpha}(h)\ ,

(5.2)

with
2d time
\tau
playing the role of
RG time
t=\log\mu
.
We observe this new ‘integrability–RG flow’ connection
in a number of non-trivial examples but will not give a general proof of it.

One may wonder how such
time-dependent models (where, e.g.,
energy is no longer conserved)
can be integrable. In fact, integrable examples of time-dependent 1d
mechanical models
are known (see, e.g.,
[
240
]
and refs. there).
Our present interest in such 2d models is motivated by the desire to find new solvable examples
of
conformal
\sigma
-models representing consistent string backgrounds with Minkowski signature.
One class of examples would be conformal
\sigma
-models with
(D+2)
-dimensional
target space metric

ds^{2}={\rm G}_{mn}(x)dx^{m}dx^{n}=-2dudv+K(u,x)du^{2}+dx^{i}dx^{i}
(
x^{m}=(u,v,x^{i})
,
i=1,...,D
),
admitting a covariantly constant null Killing vector.
In the light-cone (l.c.) gauge
u=\tau
, one then gets
a
model with a time-dependent potential
K(\tau,x)
, which is formally solvable in some cases if

K
is quadratic in
x^{i}
(see, e.g.,
[
241
]
and refs. there).
65
65

65

Another solvable example obtained as a certain Yang-Baxter deformation of flat 4d Minkowski space was found in
[
242
]
.
Examples with
K
not depending on
\tau
corresponding to
integrable l.c.-gauge theories (sine-Gordon, Liouville,
etc.) were discussed, e.g., in

[
243
,
244
,
245
]
.

The most general metric with a covariantly constant
null Killing vector has the Brinkmann-Walker form,

ds^{2}=-2dudv+K(u,x)du^{2}+A_{i}(u,x)dudx^{i}+{\rm G}_{ij}(u,x)dx^{i}dx^{j}
. Once one relaxes the assumption that
{\rm G}_{ij}
is flat, the
K
and
A_{i}

terms can be absorbed into
{\rm G}_{ij}(u,x)
by a coordinate transformation
to give

ds^{2}=-2dudv+{\rm G}_{ij}(u,x)dx^{i}dx^{j}
.
We shall focus on the remarkable class of string
\sigma
-models on such backgrounds
[
239
,
246
]
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=-2\partial_{+}u\partial_{-}v+{\rm G}_{ij}(u,x)\partial_{+}x^{i}\partial_{-}x^{j}\ .

(5.3)

Moving to a curved 2d background
h_{\mu\nu}
and incuding also a dilaton term
\mathcal{L}_{\phi}=-\alpha^{\prime}\sqrt{-h}\,R^{(2)}\phi(v,u,x)
,
this
\sigma
-model is Weyl-invariant, i.e.
\bar{\beta}_{mn}=R_{mn}+2D_{m}D_{n}\phi+...=0
,
provided
\phi
is linear in the null coordinate
v

and
{\rm G}_{ij}(u,x)
depends on
u
according to the RG equation of the ‘transverse’
\sigma
-model

\mathcal{L}={\rm G}_{ij}(x)\partial_{+}x^{i}\partial_{-}x^{j}

[
239
,
246
]
,

\displaystyle\partial_{u}{\rm G}_{ij}=\bar{\beta}_{ij}({\rm G})=\beta_{ij}+2D_{i}D_{j}\bar{\phi}\ ,\qquad\quad\beta_{ij}=R_{ij}+\ldots\ ,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\phi=v+\bar{\phi}(u,x)\ .

(5.5)

The ‘first-order’ equation (
5.4
) comes from the contribution of the connection in the
D_{m}D_{n}\phi
term
in the Weyl-invariance condition due to the null Killing form of the metric and the
v
-term in the dilaton.
66
66

66

The exact
RG evolution equation (
5.4
) in the l.c. direction
u

may be compared to an approximate RG equation
(see, e.g.,
[
247
]
) appearing in the
case of a ‘cosmological’ metric
and a time-dependent dilaton which provides a ‘friction’ term in the string
generalization of the Einstein equations.

We have ignored an additional
diffeomorphism term
D_{(i}W_{j)}
in (
5.4
) that appears only at higher loop orders.
The model (
5.3
) also admits a natural generalization to the case of non-zero ‘transverse’

{\rm B}
-field coupling
{\rm B}_{ij}(u,x)
: the resulting
(D+2)
-dimensional
\sigma
-model is Weyl invariant
provided
\partial_{u}({\rm G}_{ij}+{\rm B}_{ij})=\bar{\beta}_{ij}({\rm G},{\rm B})

where
\bar{\beta}_{ij}
is the corresponding Weyl anomaly coefficient

[
239
,
246
]
.

One example of such a conformal background with a sphere
S^{D}
as the transverse space is (to 1-loop order)

ds^{2}=-2dudv+u\,d\Omega^{2}_{D},\ \ \phi=v+\tfrac{D-24}{6}u+\frac{1}{8}D\log u
.

This construction is also naturally generalized to supersymmetric
\sigma
-models
[
246
]
. On certain
special
Kähler symmetric spaces, the supersymmetric
\sigma
-models (which then have
\mathcal{N}=(2,2)
supersymmetry
[
89
]
)
are known to have a 1-loop exact
\beta
-function
[
85
]
.
67
67

67

In general,
\mathcal{N}=(2,2)
supersymmetric
\sigma
-models on Kähler manifolds have a non-trivial 4-loop
\beta
-function coefficient
[
149
,
86
]
(which may be absorbed into a non-local redefinition of the metric in the case of Ricci-flat Kähler (Calabi-Yau) manifolds
[
84
]
).

Hence, taking one of those as the transverse theory, the background in (
5.3
),(
5.4
),(
5.5
) resulting from the 1-loop RG flow will be
exact
string backgrounds
[
246
]
.

Starting with the classical string
\sigma
-model (
5.3
) in conformal gauge and fixing the residual conformal symmetry by the l.c. gauge condition
u=\tau
, we get a time-dependent
theory for the transverse coordinates
68
68

68

Here
we ignore
the ‘diffeomorphism vector’
term that will also contribute to the time dependence of the metric; however,
this term can be eliminated or modified as desired by an appropriate field redefinition
in (
5.6
)
depending on time,
x^{i}\to y^{i}(x,\tau)
(see discussion in Section
5.1
below).

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{l.c.}={\rm G}_{ij}(\tau,x)\partial_{+}x^{i}\partial_{-}x^{j}\ ,\qquad\qquad\partial_{\tau}{\rm G}_{ij}=\beta_{ij}({\rm G})\ .

(5.6)

If we now assume that the transverse
\sigma
-model

\mathcal{L}={\rm G}_{ij}(x)\partial_{+}x^{i}\partial_{-}x^{j}
is classically
integrable, then the fact that
{\rm G}_{ij}(\tau,x)
is subject to the RG equation in (
5.6
)
suggests, in view of the above ‘integrability–RG flow’ connection,
that the l.c. gauge theory (
5.6
) is classically integrable
(admits a flat Lax connection)
and thus may potentially be solvable.

Let us note that a special class of the
\sigma
-models (
5.3
) with
{\rm G}_{ij}(u,x)=u\,g_{ij}(x)

are obtained upon fixing the conformal gauge
h_{\mu\nu}d\xi^{\mu}d\xi^{\nu}=e^{-2v}\eta_{\mu\nu}
(so that
\sqrt{-h}\,R^{(2)}=-2\partial_{+}\partial_{-}v
) in
a theory of
2d dilaton gravity (with dilaton
u
) coupled to a
\sigma
-model
[
239
]
with
g_{ij}(x)
as the target space metric,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=-\sqrt{-h}\,u\big[R^{(2)}+g_{ij}(x)\,h^{\mu\nu}\,\partial_{\mu}x^{i}\partial_{\nu}x^{j}\big]\ .

(5.7)

Particular 2d models of the type (
5.7
), with
g_{ij}(x)

corresponding to specific symmetric spaces,
appear upon dimensional reduction (in 2 Killing vector directions) of 4d Einstein gravity

[
248
,
249
,
250
]
.
After solving
\partial_{+}\partial_{-}u=0
(the equation from varying
v
) to obtain
u(\tau,\sigma)=f^{+}(\xi^{+})+f^{-}(\xi^{-})
,
the resulting ‘local coupling’
\sigma
-model,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=u(\tau,\sigma)\,g_{ij}(x)\partial_{+}x^{i}\partial_{-}x^{j}\ ,\qquad\quad u(\tau,\sigma)=f^{+}(\xi^{+})+f^{-}(\xi^{-})\ ,

(5.8)

was shown, in some special cases

[
248
]
and for general symmetric spaces

[
249
]
(see also
[
250
]
), to be integrable in the sense of admitting a Lax
pair.
The spectral parameter is now replaced by a function of
\tau,\sigma
expressed in terms of
f^{+}(\xi^{+})
,
f^{-}(\xi^{-})
and an integration constant
w
(which may be viewed as a new spectral parameter —
see below).

Here will go beyond this related earlier work in two important ways:
(i) showing that there are other integrable
\sigma
-models with ‘local couplings’ (e.g., related to integrable-deformed models)
for which the dependence of
{\rm G}_{ij}(u,x)
on
u(\tau,\sigma)
is not simply through an overall factor as in (
5.8
);
(ii) establishing the link between the dependence on
u
required for
integrability and the RG evolution of couplings in the original
\sigma
-model defined by the transverse metric
{\rm G}_{ij}(u,x)\big|_{u={\rm const}}
.

The rest of this chapter is organized as follows.
In Section
5.1
we will show that, starting from a certain class of classically integrable
\sigma
-models
admitting a flat Lax connection, it is possible to find a Lax connection for the corresponding
time-dependent theory obtained from the original one by replacing the couplings
by functions of
\tau
satisfying the 1-loop RG equations. We will explicitly discuss the case of the PCM, and a similar statement is
also true for several classes of integrable
\sigma
-models (PCM with WZ term, symmetric spaces,

\eta
-model,
\lambda
-models) presented in Table
1
.

In Section
5.2
we will demonstrate that the converse is also true: demanding the existence of a Lax pair for the time-dependent model implies that the couplings must solve the 1-loop RG equations
of the original theory. Details of the derivation of the RG flow will be presented in Appendix
E
.

In Section
5.3
we will discuss how the monodromy matrix associated with
the Lax connection can be used to construct, at least in principle, non-local
conserved charges for time-dependent models.
69
69

69

See also Section 5 of
[
4
]
for an attempt to construct higher-spin, local charges for the PCM with a local (
\sigma
-dependent) coupling.

We will concentrate on the PCM example
with comments on other models relegated to Appendix
F
.
To shed light on the notion of integrability in the time-dependent case we
will also consider consistent 1d
reductions of some simple 2d
\sigma
-models, investigating their solvability.
In Appendix
G
we will further discuss a particular linear 1d model which is a special case
of a time-dependent harmonic oscillator.

Section
5.4
contains a discussion of the results and possible extensions.
In particular, we will mention that the relation between integrability of the time-dependent
theory and the RG flow should extend to models with potentials, illustrating this
on the example of the sine-Gordon model (see also Appendix
H
).

5.1
Lax connection for time-dependent
sigma models

Below we shall discuss how,
starting from a classically integrable model (with Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}
) admitting a Lax pair
L_{\pm}
,
it is possible to find also a Lax pair
\widehat{L}_{\pm}
for the corresponding
time-dependent theory
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}
obtained from the original one
by replacing the couplings
h_{\alpha}
by functions
h_{\alpha}(\tau)
depending on
\tau

according to the 1-loop RG equation (
5.2
),
70
70

70

Here and below for simplicity
we are only indicating the dependence of the Lagrangian on the couplings, suppressing dependence on fields.

\displaystyle h_{\alpha}\to h_{\alpha}(\tau)\ ,\qquad\qquad\tfrac{d}{d\tau}h_{\alpha}(\tau)=\beta_{\alpha}(h(\tau))\ ,

(5.9)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(h_{\alpha})\ \to\ \widehat{\mathcal{L}}=\mathcal{L}(h_{\alpha}(\tau))\ .

(5.10)

The Lax connection must be modified to account for ‘extra’ terms in the equations of motion following from (
5.10
) that are proportional to the derivatives
\partial_{\tau}h_{\alpha}
of the couplings.
We propose a very simple ansatz for this modification: the extra terms can be effectively ‘absorbed’
into the spectral parameter
z

with the structure
of the Lax connection remaining the same, i.e.

\displaystyle L_{\pm}(h_{\alpha},z)\to\widehat{L}_{\pm}=L_{\pm}(h_{\alpha}(\tau),z(w;\tau,\sigma))\ ,

(5.11)

\displaystyle z\to z=z(w;\tau,\sigma)\ ,\ \ \ \qquad h_{\alpha}\to h_{\alpha}(\tau)\ .

(5.12)

While
z
was regarded as constant
in the original Lax connection,
it is now promoted to a particular function
z(w;\tau,\sigma)
of the 2d coordinates.
Here
w
is an arbitrary (complex) constant playing the role of a new spectral parameter.
As we shall see below, the explicit form of the function
z(w;\tau,\sigma)
will be model-dependent.

Let us note that if the original theory is a
\sigma
-model on flat 2d Minkowski space,
which
is classically invariant under the 2d conformal transformations
\xi^{\pm}\to f^{\pm}(\xi^{\pm})
,
then the Lagrangian of the corresponding time-dependent theory (
5.10
) changes
under such transformations as

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}=\mathcal{L}(h_{\alpha}(\tau))\ \to\ \mathcal{L}(h_{\alpha}(\tau^{\prime}))\ ,\qquad\ \ \ \ \ \tau=\xi^{+}+\xi^{-}\ \to\ \tau^{\prime}=f^{+}(\xi^{+})+f^{-}(\xi^{-})\ .

(5.13)

In the case of the
string

\sigma
-model (
5.3
),(
5.6
),
this is equivalent to replacing the l.c. gauge
u=\tau
with a
more general one given by the solution
u=f^{+}(\xi^{+})+f^{-}(\xi^{-})
of the
equation of motion
\partial_{+}\partial_{-}u=0
.
One special choice is
u=\xi^{-}
for which
we get

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm ch}=\mathcal{L}(h_{\alpha}(\xi^{-}))
.
One may formally consider that theory,
which only fixes one ‘half’ of the conformal reparametrizations, as an extreme limit
(f^{+}(\xi^{+}),f^{-}(\xi^{-}))=(0,\xi^{-})
of (
5.13
).

To provide evidence for the above proposal, we have considered
six examples of integrable
\sigma
-models (each introduced in Section
1.4
) with the results summarized in Table
1
below.
There, for each model, we give the original Lagrangian, original Lax pair, 1-loop RG equation and its solution, and the expression for the function
z(w;\tau,\sigma)
that generalizes the original spectral parameter in such a way
that (
5.11
) gives a Lax pair for the time-dependent theory (
5.10
).

The classically integrable
\sigma
-models in Table
1
are each 1-loop renormalizable
assuming a particular choice of
‘renormalizable’
diffeomorphism vector. This means that the insertion of time dependence according to the RG flow in (
5.9
),(
5.10
)
will only apply to a finite set of couplings.
As discussed
at the beginning of this chapter,
a time-dependent
\sigma
-model
arises in the light-cone gauge from a conformal string
\sigma
-model.
There, the time dependence is governed by the RG flow with
a specific diffeomorphism vector (generally different from the renormalizable one)
following from the
(D+2)
-dimensional dilaton solving the Weyl-invariance condition.
However,
these two patterns of time dependence
(according to the RG flow with different diffeomorphism vectors)
are simply related by a time-dependent field redefinition in
the corresponding time-dependent models, and such a redefinition preserves the existence of a Lax representation.

All of our examples are built on either a group
G
or a symmetric space
G/H
. Our notation is the same as that of Section
1.4
, except that here
h
denotes a coupling constant, or
h_{\alpha}
to denote many of them (whereas before they were called
{\rm h}
to distinguish from group elements).
We again denote
J_{\pm}=g^{-1}\partial_{\pm}g
, where
g\in G
is scalar field.
PCM
k
stands for the principal chiral model plus a Wess-Zumino (
{\rm B}
-field)
term with coefficient
k

(and the special case
h=\pm k
is the conformal WZW model).
The group space
\eta
-model is an integrable deformation of the PCM
while the group space and symmetric space
\lambda
-models are integrable
deformations of the WZW and gauged WZW models.

PCM

PCM
k

Lagrangian

\mathcal{L}=-\tfrac{1}{2}h{\rm Tr}[J_{+}J_{-}]

\mathcal{L}=-\tfrac{1}{2}h{\rm Tr}[J_{+}J_{-}]+k\,\mathcal{L}_{\rm WZ}

Lax connection

L_{\pm}=\tfrac{1}{2}(1+z^{\pm 1})J_{\pm}

L_{\pm}=\tfrac{1}{2}(1+z^{\pm 1})(1\pm\frac{k}{h})J_{\pm}

1-loop RG equation

and its solution

\begin{matrix}[l]\frac{d}{dt}h=c\,\\
h(t)=c\,t\end{matrix}

\begin{matrix}[l]\frac{d}{dt}h=c\,(1-\frac{k^{2}}{h^{2}}),\ \ \ \frac{d}{dt}k=0\\
h(t)-k\tanh^{-1}{\big(\frac{h(t)}{k}\big)}=c\,t\end{matrix}

Spectral parameter

function

z=\sqrt{\frac{w+\sigma-\tau}{w+\sigma+\tau}}

\begin{matrix}[l]c\,(w+\sigma-\tau)+2zh(\tau)\,\frac{k+z(k+h(\tau))}{k(1+z)^{2}+h(\tau)(z^{2}-1)}\\
\quad\quad\qquad+k\log{\big(\frac{(1+z)(k-h(\tau))}{(k-h(\tau))+z(k+h(\tau))}\big)}=0\end{matrix}

Group space
\eta
-model

Group space
\lambda
-model

Lagrangian

\mathcal{L}=-\tfrac{1}{2}h{\rm Tr}[J_{+}\frac{1}{1-\varkappa{\mathcal{R}}}J_{-}]

\begin{matrix}[l]\mathcal{L}=k\big[\mathcal{L}_{G/G}(g,A)\\
\qquad\qquad-(\lambda^{-1}-1){\rm Tr}(A_{+}A_{-})\big]\end{matrix}

Lax connection

\begin{matrix}[l]L_{\pm}=\tfrac{1}{2}(1+z^{\pm 1})C_{\pm}\\
C_{\pm}=-(1+\varkappa^{2})\text{Ad}_{g}\frac{1}{1\pm\varkappa{\mathcal{R}}}J_{\pm}\end{matrix}

\begin{matrix}[l]L_{\pm}=\tfrac{1}{2}(1+z^{\pm 1})\frac{2}{1+\lambda}A_{\pm}\\
A_{\pm}\text{ takes on-shell value}\end{matrix}

1-loop RG equation

and its solution

\begin{matrix}[l]\frac{d}{dt}h=c\,(1+\varkappa^{2})^{2},\ \ \frac{d}{dt}(h\varkappa^{-1})=0\\
\nu\equiv\tfrac{h(t)}{4\varkappa(t)}=\text{const}\\
{\tan}^{-1}\,{\varkappa(t)}+\frac{\varkappa(t)}{1+\varkappa(t)^{2}}=\frac{c\,}{2\nu}t\end{matrix}

\begin{matrix}[l]\frac{d}{dt}\lambda=-\frac{2c\,}{k}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{(1+\lambda)^{2}},\ \frac{d}{dt}k=0,\\
-2\log{\lambda(t)}-\lambda(t)+\lambda(t)^{-1}=\frac{2c\,}{k}t\end{matrix}

Spectral parameter

function

\begin{matrix}[l]\frac{c\,}{2\nu}(w+\sigma)-\frac{1-z}{1+z}\frac{\varkappa(\tau)}{1+\varkappa(\tau)^{2}}\\
\qquad\qquad+\tan^{-1}{\big(\frac{1}{\varkappa(\tau)}\frac{1-z}{1+z}\big)}=0\end{matrix}

\begin{matrix}[l]\frac{2c\,}{k}(w+\sigma)+\frac{1-z}{1+z}[\lambda(\tau)-\lambda(\tau)^{-1}]\\
\qquad\qquad-2\log{\big(\frac{z\lambda(\tau)-1}{z-\lambda(\tau)}\big)}=0\end{matrix}

Symmetric space
\sigma
-model

Symmetric space
\lambda
-model

Lagrangian

\mathcal{L}=-\tfrac{1}{2}h{\rm Tr}[J_{+}P_{G/H}J_{-}]

In this section we shall explicitly consider only the basic PCM example,

For the PCM corresponding to a simple Lie group
G

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=-\tfrac{1}{2}h{\rm Tr}[J_{+}J_{-}]\ ,\qquad J=g^{-1}dg\ ,\ \ g\in G\ ,

(5.14)

the global
G\times G
symmetry implies
that only the overall coupling
h
runs under the 1-loop
RG,

The constant
c\equiv c_{{}_{G}}
is the dual Coxeter number of the group
G
(see Appendix

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}h=c\,\ ,\qquad\qquad h(t)=c\,t\ ,\qquad\qquad c\,\equiv c_{{}_{G}}.

(5.15)

The corresponding time-dependent theory
(

) is then
given
by

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}=-\tfrac{1}{2}c\,\tau\,{\rm Tr}[J_{+}J_{-}]\ .

(5.16)

As explained above, this theory arises naturally in the l.c. gauge
u=\tau
from the conformal string
\sigma
-model (cf. (

),(

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=-2\partial_{+}u\partial_{-}v-\tfrac{1}{2}c\,u{\rm Tr}[J_{+}J_{-}]\ ,

(5.17)

where the constant
c\,
can be eliminated by a rescaling
(u,v)\to(c\,^{-1}u,\,c\,v)
.

The equations of motion for the model (
5.16
) written in first-order form are

\displaystyle\partial_{+}(\tau J_{-})+\partial_{-}(\tau J_{+})=0\ ,

(5.18)

\displaystyle F_{+-}(J)\equiv\partial_{+}J_{-}-\partial_{-}J_{+}+[J_{+},J_{-}]=0\ .

(5.19)

Starting from the Lax connection of the original PCM (
5.14
)

\displaystyle L_{\pm}=\tfrac{1}{2}(1+z^{\pm 1})J_{\pm}\ ,

(5.20)

and making the replacement

z\to\sqrt{\frac{{w+\sigma-\tau}}{{w+\sigma+\tau}}}
where
w
is a constant spectral parameter
(see Table
1
), we obtain the following expression for the
Lax connection (
5.11
) of the time-dependent theory

\displaystyle\widehat{L}_{\pm}=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(1+{{z}\,}^{\pm 1}\big)J_{\pm}\ ,\qquad\qquad{z}=\sqrt{\frac{{w+\sigma-\tau}}{{w+\sigma+\tau}}}\ .

(5.21)

Indeed,
its curvature is

\displaystyle F_{+-}(\widehat{L})\equiv\partial_{+}\widehat{L}_{-}-\partial_{-}\widehat{L}_{+}+[\widehat{L}_{+},\widehat{L}_{-}]

(5.22)

\displaystyle\qquad\ \ \ \,\,=\frac{{z}}{2(w+\sigma-\tau)}\Big[\partial_{+}(\tau J_{-})+\partial_{-}(\tau J_{+})\Big]+\tfrac{1}{4}(1+{z})(1+{{z}\,}^{-1})\ F_{+-}(J)\ ,

so that, at
any
(\tau,\sigma)
, its vanishing for all
w
implies
the equations of motion (
5.18
),(
5.19
) of the time-dependent theory.

A surprising feature
(shared also by the other examples in Table
1
)
is that, despite the Lagrangian (
5.16
) explicitly involving only
\tau
,
the Lax connection (
5.21
)
also depends on the 2d spatial coordinate
\sigma
.
The spatial coordinate
\sigma
and the spectral parameter
w
appear only through
the combination
w+\sigma
. Since
w
is a complex constant, it cannot be
eliminated by a shift of real
\sigma
. Moreover, what is important is the
existence of a Lax connection depending on
w
, which can in principle be used
to construct conserved charges, etc. Indeed, we can instead interpret the
formal possibility to shift
\sigma
as the freedom to introduce a spectral
parameter in the first place.

Another unusual property (shared by all the examples) is that the Lax connection has branch cuts in the spectral
w
-plane
(e.g., for the PCM the branch cuts end at
w=-\sigma\pm\tau
).
Normally, one could simply remove square roots by redefining the spectral parameter (or more formally moving to an appropriate Riemann surface on which the Lax connection is meromorphic).
However, this is not possible since the positions of the branch cuts depend on
(\tau,\sigma)
and one cannot redefine the spectral parameter in a way depending on
(\tau,\sigma)
without changing the equations of motion encoded in the zero curvature condition.

In each case in Table
1
, one can freely choose any branch of the function
z(w;\tau,\sigma)
(in the
w
-plane) while still encoding the correct equations of motion in the zero-curvature equation. For example, in the PCM case, one may choose either sign of the square root
{z}=\pm\sqrt{\tfrac{{w+\sigma-\tau}}{{w+\sigma+\tau}}}
in the Lax connection (
5.21
).
All similar square roots in the spectral functions below
have the same branch ambiguity, corresponding to the option to reverse their sign.

Applying a general conformal transformation to the action corresponding to (
5.16
),(
5.21
) we get (cf. (
5.13
))

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}=-\tfrac{1}{2}c\,\,\big(f^{+}(\xi^{+})+f^{-}(\xi^{-})\big)\,{\rm Tr}[J_{+}J_{-}]\ ,\qquad

(5.23)

\displaystyle\widehat{L}_{\pm}=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\,1+z^{\pm 1}\big)J_{\pm}\ ,\qquad\quad z=\sqrt{\frac{{w-2f^{-}(\xi^{-})}}{{w+2f^{+}(\xi^{+})}}}\ .

(5.24)

This theory may equivalently be obtained from the string
\sigma
-model (
5.17
)
by picking up a generalized l.c. gauge
u=\tau^{\prime}=f^{+}(\xi^{+})+f^{-}(\xi^{-})
.
A (degenerate) limit
u=a+b\tau
,
a\to 1
,
b\to 0
eliminates the explicit
\tau
-dependence and gives back the original PCM (
5.14
), with (
5.23
) becoming
the original Lax connection in (
5.20
) with a redefined spectral parameter
z\to\sqrt{\frac{{w-2a_{-}}}{{w+2a_{+}}}}
, where
a_{\pm}
are constants satisfying
a_{+}+a_{-}=1
.
We note that the theory (
5.23
) is a special case of (
5.8
) where
{\rm G}_{ij}
is the group-space metric. Indeed, an equivalent expression for the Lax connection
of this ‘local’ PCM (or symmetric space
\sigma
-model, see Table

)
was originally found in
[
249
]

where the dependence of the analog of the
spectral parameter on the functions
f^{+}(\xi^{+})
and

was
discovered. The spectral function

in (

) depends separately on

and its dual field
\tilde{u}=f^{+}(\xi^{+})-f^{-}(\xi^{-})
,
du=*d\tilde{u}

and thus separately on

and
